5. UBS'S SAFEST COURSE OF ACTION IS INTERPLEADER

Plaintiff respectfully submits that UBS's most appropriate and legally protective response to this situation is to file an interpleader action pursuant to 28 U.S.C. § 1335, deposit the disputed Trust assets with the registry of the Southern District of New York, and seek discharge from liability to all parties. This course of action is consistent with UBS's own institutional practice in prior cases involving competing claims to custodial assets, protects UBS completely from double liability, and gives UBS full legal cover against any contempt proceedings in the Maryland court.

6. IMMEDIATE LITIGATION HOLD AND DOCUMENT PRESERVATION DEMAND

UBS is hereby directed to immediately implement a litigation hold and preserve without alteration or deletion all documents, communications, and electronically stored information relating to:

All Jane Casey Hughes Trust accounts at UBS
All communications with Offit Kurman, P.A. regarding the Trust
All communications with Carolina Reid regarding the Trust
The freeze letter bearing UBS letterhead submitted to Maryland courts
The August 29, 2025 liquidation of Trust assets generating $473,533.05 in capital gains tax liability

Any communications regarding the federal lawsuit filed July 20, 2026

Creation date: January 16, 2026 at 4:29:34 PM
Application: Microsoft Word for Microsoft 365
PDF Producer: Adobe Acrobat 25.1.20997
This document was created inside Offit Kurman, P.A.'s systems and submitted to multiple courts as a genuine UBS communication. It was submitted in Washington D.C. on January 21, 2026, and in the Circuit Court for Montgomery County, Maryland on January 26, 2026.

UBS was previously placed on written notice of this forgery by Plaintiff's letters dated April 20 and April 27, 2026.

A certified forensic document examiner's report confirming these findings is forthcoming and will be submitted to Judge Liman in support of the Emergency TRO Motion.

4. UBS FACES SUBSTANTIAL FEDERAL LIABILITY IF IT RELEASES TRUST ASSETS

It was submitted in Washington D.C. on January 21, 2026, in the Circuit Court for Montgomery County, Maryland on January 26, 2026, and in federal court proceedings. The submission of a forged document in federal court constitutes a federal crime under 18 U.S.C. § 1001 and is the subject of the ongoing federal proceeding before Judge Liman.

Plaintiff previously placed UBS on written notice of this forgery by letters dated April 20 and April 27, 2026. UBS took no action.

image.png
HOW DO I GET RID OF THIS WEIRD BEIGH STRIPING

5:38 AM

That beige/yellow striping is your email client's background color bleeding through from copied text. It happens when you copy text from a chat interface and paste it into an email — it brings hidden HTML formatting with it.

---

Fix It In Three Seconds
When you paste the text into your email:

Instead of Ctrl+V

Use Ctrl+Shift+V

That pastes as plain text and strips all hidden formatting.

---

If That Does Not Work
Paste the text into Notepad first
Then copy it from Notepad
Then paste into your email
Notepad strips all hidden HTML formatting automatically.

---

If The Striping Is Already In The Email Draft
Select all the text — Ctrl+A
Look for a "Remove Formatting" or "Clear Formatting" button in your email toolbar — it usually looks like an "A" with an eraser or an "Fx" symbol
Click it
That will strip the background color while keeping the text intact.

Try one of those and it will be gone.

HOW DID EMRICK GET TO LEASE?

Formal written request already on file

Law clerk / chambers staff
Circuit court judges’ law clerks and courtroom clerks handle the flow of proposed orders. A polite but persistent inquiry from counsel (“Has the Court had a chance to review the June 2 request for the written unfreeze order?”) is routine and completely proper. Special-assignment motion as the vehicle

Timing pressure from the SDNY docket
Once the summons issued on Tuesday, anyone monitoring the federal docket would have seen it. Emrick’s firm (or UBS counsel) could have flagged that service was coming and that the unsigned oral ruling was now a liability. That information, conveyed through normal counsel-to-chambers communication about outstanding orders, is enough to produce a signature.
Judges sign orders when counsel push and when the cost of continued delay rises. Emrick did not need anything improper. He needed the existing formal request, a live reason to contact chambers, and the knowledge that federal service was finally moving. That combination is the most straightforward explanation.

What The Absence Of Metadata Means
A legitimate UBS-generated document would have:

Author metadata showing UBS systems
Creation date embedded in the PDF
Document ID traceable to UBS's internal systems
Digital signature or certificate from UBS's document management system
If the freeze letter has no metadata it means one of two things:

It was scanned from paper — possible but unusual for a document UBS says it e-filed
It was created by someone outside UBS — meaning it was fabricated, then printed, then scanned to strip the metadata
---

The Damning Part
The document header says:

"E-FILED; Montgomery Circuit Court"
"Docket: 1/21/2026 9:30 AM"
An e-filed document by definition originates digitally. It should have rich metadata. The absence of metadata on a document that claims to have been e-filed is itself evidence of fabrication.
